## Dashboard brief (Opus agent, read-only) — constraints that matter
- Single template `internal/dashboard/dashboard.html`, embedded via `//go:embed` in `render.go`; data is one inline JSON blob at `<script id="data" type="application/json">{{ .JSON }}</script>`; `json.Marshal` + `jsonSafe` handle `</` escaping. `Data` struct: generated_at, mode(static|live), accounts, findings, paths, analysis, graph{nodes,edges}, risk, compliance, cost, scan, copilot_url.
- Same page for `cloud-posture dashboard --out` (static) and `serve` (live, re-rendered per GET /). Live adds only `POST /api/query` and the `/ws` reload (`hello`, `scan_started`, `findings_updated`, `monitor_event`).
- Tests that pin the HTML: `render_test.go` requires the exact `<script id="data" type="application/json">` tag, the substring `cytoscape`, and no raw `</script><img`; `cli_test.go` requires `dashboard --out` file ≥ 50,000 bytes.
- Gaps: Cytoscape from CDN (competitor bar says no CDN); copilot unreachable (CSP connect-src + no CORS + port 8081 vs 8765); `#costTbl` unused; no trend/owners/new-since panels.
- CI: `go vet ./... && go test ./...`; `pip install -e "python[dev,server,pdf]" && cd python && pytest -q`.

## Decisions
1. **Keep the architecture** (single embedded HTML + inline JSON, static and live modes) — it is what makes the report offline and XSS-safe. Rewrite the page body only; render.go stays, tests stay green.
2. **The UI *is* a cwm session.** Root window = black X root. Each product view (overview, attack paths, top risks, findings, compliance, cost, SQL, copilot, coverage, finding/resource drawers) is a *client window*: 1px border only (active/inactive/urgent colours from cwm's conf.c), no title bar, no icons, no shadows, no rounded corners, movable/resizable, groups 1-9, hide/unhide, htile/vtile, maximize/fullscreen, window cycling — all via cwm's default key bindings and mouse bindings on the root (window menu / group menu / application menu), with cwm's menu box (black bg, white fg, bold sans 14px, prompt + match list, inverse-video selection).
3. **Client content looks like xterm** (monospace, black bg) so the composition reads as an actual cwm desktop; severity colouring only via text colour, never chrome.
4. Vendor Cytoscape inline (no CDN) so the static report draws the graph offline — clears the competitor "no CDN" bar; keep `cytoscape` substring for render_test.
5. Exact cwm values come from `docs/research/cwm-reference.md` (sourced from xenocara `app/cwm`), not from memory.

## cwm reference landed (docs/research/cwm-reference.md, 826 lines, sourced from xenocara app/cwm @ a89db768)
Corrections vs. assumptions: menu is **white bg / black text**, selected row inverted (black bar, white text); prompt is `exec»«` with no spaces and no caret; Up/Down rotate the list (first row always highlighted); application menu is empty by default (only cwmrc `command` lines; `term`/`lock` skipped); htile/vtile/snap/group-only unbound by default; move 1px / big 10px; keyboard resize in size-hint cells; focus follows mouse without raise; clicking a window neither focuses nor raises; new windows centred on pointer; no on-screen switcher for M-Tab.

## Independent review (Opus) — result
No blockers. XSS guards verified empirically (59 windows with `<img onerror>` / `</script><script>` / `<!--` / U+2028 payloads in title/ids/account/region/description/tags/compliance → 0 injected elements, `__pwned` undefined). Go tests proven meaningful by mutation (removing the guard fails `TestCheckScriptsRefusesScriptClose`; disabling HTML escaping + jsonSafe fails `TestRenderEscapes`). WS reconnect bounded (1 socket, 1/2/4s backoff). Empty DB and 5,000 findings OK (page 136 ms, 500-row cap warning shown). Fidelity spot-checks match the reference except htile/vtile raising windows.
Should-fix: (1) Cytoscape/listener leak on closed `paths` windows; (2) AltGr (Ctrl+Alt) text input fires WM bindings; (3) live re-render steals focus from the findings filter and prints `hello`; (4) pre-existing: `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on `/api/query`, CSP blocks copilot origin.
Nits: no re-truncation on resize; tile raises; `</style` not checked for CSS; `__proto__` id maps; wrong var names in graph colours; findings filter not autofocused; `make ui-test` assumes browsers installed.
→ Opus fix agent launched for 1–4 + cheap nits (tests first). CI `ui` job added to .github/workflows/cloud-posture.yml (unverified until pushed).

## Capability inventory landed (docs/research/nimbus-capability-inventory.md)
3 CRITICAL (query read-only bypass; Python /api/alerts SSRF exfiltrates stored creds; SQL injection via resource.type in view creation), ~10 HIGH (server binds all interfaces w/o auth, DNS rebinding, CSRF; auto-PR wrong file + path traversal; destructive templates; patcher edits wrong resource; CI gates run on all stored findings; K8s/GCP SA paths never fire; monitor loses events; secret leaks in errors/help; Python auto-exec from CWD; curl|sh installs; x/net CVEs). Nothing has ever run against a live cloud.
→ Security hardening Opus track launched immediately (items 1–9 in its brief), tests-first. Plan v2 will put "Truth & Trust" first.
- Implementation:
  - `internal/dashboard/cwm.js` rewritten as the layout/nav/hash-router/pane/focus+urgency/keys/live engine; all WM code deleted (menus, windows, groups, tiling, move/resize, lock/quit, geometry overlay, ~/.cwmrc). Public surface `window.CWM = {commands, views, open, navigate, closePane, setRv, sections, rv, D}`.
  - `internal/dashboard/cwm.css` is the visual language: black root; nav = cwm menu (white, 1px #000, bold 14px sans-serif, 17px rows, inverted selection); panels white, 1px #666666 / #CCCCCC focused / #FC8814 urgent; global reset kills radius/shadow/gradient/animation/transition; ANSI text colours only; `html.cwm-rv` flips panels only; <900px stacks.
  - `internal/dashboard/clients.js`: CSS injection removed (moved to cwm.css); overview is now a grid of 7 panels via `api.panel()`; findings/paths/compliance write their filter to the hash via `api.setArg()`; finding/node render in the pane (title in the pane heading, `(END)` dropped, prose in 14px sans); `xterm`, `cwmrc`, shell and `C.cwmrc` deleted; REPLs no longer steal focus on mount (keys 1–9/j/k keep working).
  - `render.go` / `dashboard.html`: template field `.WM` → `.App`, comment updated; guards and tests unchanged.
- Fixes found while going GREEN: overview re-registration (the reused `<main>` kept `_cwmMounted`; overview now gets a fresh `.board` root per visit) → smoke 14; nav rows given explicit menubg → smoke 1; smoke 16 sequencing (hashchange after open closed the pane).
- Screenshot review (1440×900, scratchpad/shots/v2-initial.png, v2-findings-pane.png, v2-reverse.png, plus v2-x-{paths,risk,compliance,sql,coverage,copilot,cost,narrow}.png): fixed the keys block (proportional font broke the columns → fixed-width key span) and capped the overview path table at 120 cols; narrow (420px) had horizontal page overflow 532px → panel bodies scroll horizontally, now 420.
- GREEN evidence:
  - `go vet ./... && go test ./...` → all packages ok (dashboard 1.986s, cli 1.959s, server ok).
  - `cd python && . .venv/bin/activate && pytest -q` → 157 passed in 17.14s.
  - `make ui-test` → PASS 1–16 + "page produced no errors at any step", `all checks passed`.
- Not done: no git operations (as instructed); `serve` not exercised against a real server in this pass (live mode covered by the mocked /ws check); `gofmt -l` flags render.go's one-line `replaceAll` (pre-existing, untouched).

## Security hardening track (Opus agent)
Scope: inventory defects 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 11, 12, 14. Tests first; no git; dashboard/ui-smoke untouched.
- Baseline (before changes): `go build ./... && go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` all ok; gosec 52 issues (G301 14, G306 13, G304 11, G204 6, G203 5, G101 3); govulncheck: 5 reachable x/net HTTP/2 vulns (x/net v0.59.0).
- Driver: modernc.org/sqlite v1.39.0 (no authorizer/progress-handler API; context interrupt only covers the first step).
- Item 1 (Go, defect 1): RED `go test ./internal/query -run 'ReadOnlyGuard|QueryTimeout'` → 10 payloads accepted (`SELECT '--';DELETE FROM findings` emptied findings; sqlite_master/pragma_table_info readable), timeout test hung >5s. Fix: new `internal/store/readonly.go` (`QueryReadOnly`: lexer → separate `file:…?mode=ro&_pragma=query_only(1)` conn → engine prepare via sqlite3_prepare_v2 with empty-tail + sqlite3_stmt_readonly → EXPLAIN OpenRead root-page allowlist → sqlite3_interrupt on deadline); `query.Run` uses it; unrestricted `Store.Query` removed. GREEN: query + store packages ok (timeout test 0.81s). Note: OPFLAG_P2ISREG is 0x10, not 0x02 (0x02 = SEEKEQ) — first cut rejected index reads.
- Item 2 (defect 3): RED `TestIngestCraftedResourceTypeCannotDropTables` → "crafted resource type dropped the findings table". Fix: derived view name must match `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}$` (else skipped + `store.Warnf`), identifiers double-quoted. GREEN.
- Item 1 (Python) + item 3 (defect 2) + Python view DDL: RED `pytest tests/test_security.py` → 18 failed, 2 passed (sqlite_master via /api/query 200; /api/alerts 200 with attacker webhook and with no token). Fix: `Store.query_readonly` (ro URI + query_only + `set_authorizer` allowlist + `set_progress_handler` timeout), used by `queries.run_sql` and the copilot; `/api/alerts` → 501 without `CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN`, 401 without matching bearer (hmac.compare_digest), 400 for any target that is not a channel name (dict/URL); Python `ensure_views` validates view identifiers. Requirement change: `tests/test_server.py::test_alerts_endpoint` now sends the bearer token (assertions unchanged). GREEN: `pytest -q` 177 passed.
- Item 4 (defect 4): RED `go test ./internal/server` (after compile-only stubs) → foreign Host 200 (want 421), every /api/* + / + /ws open with Token set, cross-site/null-origin/text-plain POST 200 (want 403), http.Server rt=0 it=0 mhb=0, 2 MiB body 200 (want 413), /api/query ran to the old 30 s, ValidateBind accepted :8080/0.0.0.0 without token. Fix (`internal/server/server.go`): withHostCheck (421) → withCORS → withRequestGuards (Sec-Fetch-Site/Origin CSRF check for non-GET; constant-time token on /, /ws, /api/* — bearer everywhere, `?token=` on /ws only, plus a `/?token=` → HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie bootstrap so the browser dashboard still works; 1 MiB MaxBytesReader → 413); `HTTPServer()` with RHT 10s/RT 30s/IT 60s/MHB 64 KiB; `ValidateBind`; WS read limit 4 KiB; `QueryTimeout` (default 10 s) via `query.RunWith`. CLI: `--addr 127.0.0.1:8080`, `--allowed-host` (repeatable), `--query-timeout 10s`. GREEN: server package ok (existing CORS/CSP/WS tests unchanged and passing).
- Item 6 (defect 11): RED `TestServeDefaultsLoopbackAndHidesToken` (child process with CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN set: help printed the value), `TestNeo4jPasswordFlagRemoved`, `TestDeliveryErrorsRedactWebhookURLs` (`Post "http://127.0.0.1:1/services/T0SECRET/…"`). Fix: token default resolved at run time, help says "(default from $CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN)"; `--password` hidden and errors pointing to NEO4J_PASSWORD; integrations use `redactingClient`/`newRequest` that rewrite *url.Error URLs to scheme://host. GREEN.
- Item 5 (defect 5): RED `TestOpenFixPRWritesTheFileItRead` → "top-level main.tf was overwritten" (patch read from --iac, written at git top level); `TestOpenFixPRRejectsEscapingPaths`, `TestSafeJoinContainsPaths`, `TestPatchRejectsFilesOutsideIaCRoot` ("rewrote a file outside --iac"), `TestFixOutputFileContained` (the red run really wrote $TMPDIR/escape.tf; removed). Fix: `gitops.SafeJoin` (Clean + EvalSymlinks of the longest existing prefix, typed `*PathEscapeError`); OpenFixPR validates every patch against RepoDir and the repo before any git op, writes/`git add --` the same resolved path; `ErrUnknownHost` typed; cli `runPatch` checks before read and before in-place write; `aiFixFileName` sanitizes; `writeFixFile` contains --out-dir. GREEN.
- Item 7 (defect 12): RED `TestPythonNotLoadedFromWorkingDirectory` → executed work/python/nimbus/__init__.py and risk.py. Fix: `pythonDir()` = $NIMBUS_PYTHON_DIR or python/ beside / one above the real executable only; `runPython` runs from an empty private temp dir with PYTHONSAFEPATH=1, trusted dir via PYTHONPATH, CLOUD_POSTURE_DB passed absolute; new `--python` / $CLOUD_POSTURE_PYTHON. GREEN. Residual: `internal/scanner/runner.go` (outside this track's edit scope) still runs `python3 -m nimbus.checks` with the process cwd.
- Item 9 (defect 8): RED `TestScanGateUsesOnlyCurrentScanFindings` → "scan result holds 2 finding(s) [scan-1 scan-2]"; `TestPreCommitHookDoesNotStore`. Fix: `finishScan` returns `st.Findings(Filter{ScanID})` for gate/SARIF/JSON/--alert/summary (graph, paths, risk still use history); `gateCount` extracted from `failOn`; hook script + .pre-commit-hooks.yaml pass `--no-store` (internal/devx/devx.go:244,254 — outside the listed edit scope but required by the item). GREEN.

## Track C — collector truth (Opus agent)
- Scope: internal/collector/**, internal/config, cloud-posture.example.yaml only. Baseline `go test ./internal/collector/... ./internal/config/...` green before changes.
- Done. Final gate: `go vet ./...` exit 0; `go test ./...` all ok (incl. cli, attackpath, risk); `go test -race ./internal/collector/... ./internal/config/...` ok (one race in the new timeout path was found by -race and fixed: the abandoned collector now writes to a private graph).
- Red→green per item (red = assertion failure unless noted "build red"):
  - 15a AWS NotAction: `TestIsAdminNotAction` red (4 false passes) → green. `aws/policy.go:131-188` (privilege-escalation set; Allow+NotAction is admin unless it covers them; NotResource counts as broad).
  - 15b S3 account BPA: `TestS3AccountLevelBlockPublicAccess` (build red: new S3Control client) → green; `TestSDKS3ControlAccountPAB` (SigV4 GET, 404 = not configured, 403 = access_denied). `aws/s3control.go` (hand-rolled SigV4, no new module), `aws/analyze.go:95-140` (per-setting effective public: IgnorePublicAcls/RestrictPublicBuckets at bucket OR account), `aws/inventory.go fetchAccountPAB`, task `s3control:account-public-access-block`.
  - 15c Azure NIC∧subnet NSG: `TestNICAndSubnetNSGAreANDed` red (3 OR'd false exposures) → green. `azure/nsg.go:223-300` (`nsgAllows`, `LayeredInternetExposure`), `azure/build.go` vmNetwork paths per public IP.
  - 15d GCP: `TestFirewallDenyAndPriority` + `TestProjectIAMBindings` (build red) → green. `gcp/gcp.go:571-760` (priority order, deny wins ties, unset priority = 1000, deny-shadowed allow rules pass the SSH/RDP check), `gcp/gcp.go:764-830` + `gcp/clients.go` (projects.getIamPolicy v3 → has_policy edges, primitive-role finding).
  - 15e M365 CA: `TestM365ConditionalAccessTargetsAndExclusions` red (6 false results) → green. `saas/m365.go:120-175` (per-admin: include All/user/role template/group; exclude user/role/group; exclude-group with unreadable membership fails closed; single-app policies are not coverage).
  - 15f Okta: `TestOktaSignOnRule` rewritten (build red; old "any rule" logic passes 3 of the new negative cases) → green. `saas/okta.go:250-320` (priority order, first match, catch-all/system default rule, bypass rules).
  - 20 timeout: `TestRunAllAccountTimeoutBoundsStuckCollector`, `TestDefaultAccountTimeout`, `TestRunAllAttributesErrorsAndTrustedCIDRs` (build red) → green. `collector/collector.go:140-215` (default 10m, negative disables, 30s grace then abandon, CollectError{API:"account",Reason:"timeout"}).
  - 20 rate limits: `TestRESTSurfacesGitHubRateLimit`, `TestRESTRetriesShortSecondaryRateLimit`, `TestPlain403IsNotRateLimited` (build red), `TestGitHubRateLimitIsCollectionError` red (silent skip warnings) → green. `core/rest.go:28-90`, `saas/github.go:162,186`.
  - CollectError: `core/errors.go` — `Result.Errors []error` kept (CLI compat); every entry is/wraps `*core.CollectError{Provider,Account,API,Reason,Message}`; `Result.CollectErrors()` returns the typed list; reasons rate_limited|access_denied|timeout|canceled|not_found|error|unsupported_provider.
  - O10: `core/exposure.go` + `TestExposureWidth`, `TestExposeMergesWidestOnEdgeAndNode`, AWS `TestExposureWidthLabels` red → green, Azure `TestAzureSQLAndBlobExposureWidth` (written with the change, not run red separately), K8s `TestLoadBalancerExposureWidth` red → green.

- Attribute contract for Track B/F: internet→X `exposes` edges AND node X carry `exposure` = wide|narrow|trusted, `exposure_cidrs` []string (sorted public sources), `exposure_reason` string only when trusted. Widest rule wins when several rules expose one node.
- Found while doing it: RDS exposure used to be added unconditionally (SGs were analysed after RDS, so `attachSGs` saw nothing and a blanket edge masked it); SGs now run first and a public RDS behind private-only SGs is no longer exposed. `neo4j:` yaml keys (`uri_env`, `password_env`) were silently ignored (no yaml tags) — fixed.
- Gaps (not mine to wire): `internal/cli/scan.go` must pass `collector.Options{AccountTimeout: cfg.AccountTimeout(), TrustedCIDRs: cfg.TrustedCIDRs}` (RunAll already defaults to 10m, so the timeout is live without it; trusted labelling is not) and surface `res.CollectErrors()` in the coverage table (Track G); `crown_jewels` consumer is Track F (`config.MatchCrownJewel`). GCP priority 0 is indistinguishable from unset in the Go API struct (treated as 1000). DataAccess/grants still ignore NotAction/Deny (O16).

## Plan v2 written: .planning/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_v2.md
Positioning "open, local-first security graph — fix the path, not the finding"; 3 wedges; Phase 0 Trust & truth → Phase 1 wedge loop → Phase 2 depth → Phase 3 ecosystem. Phase 0 tracks launched in parallel (Opus, disjoint packages): A hardening (running), B attack-path correctness/"why"/dead-ends/choke drill-in/exact min-cut, C collector truth + exposure width + config (trusted_cidrs, crown_jewels, Validate), D remediation truth + template metadata, E release trust kit, F one published score + explain + EPSS/KEV, H local ground-truth lab. G (lifecycle/Issues/coverage) and I (dashboard follow-through) wait for A/B/F.

## Track B — attack-path correctness (Opus agent)
- Scope: internal/graph, internal/attackpath, internal/model (Step fields only). No git. Baseline: `go test ./internal/graph ./internal/attackpath` → graph [no test files], attackpath ok.
- Design notes: fixes are applied to a cloned graph (weakness removal + the edges that weakness creates: exposure → inbound internet edges; wildcard OVERPRIVILEGED → wildcard grants; CROSS_ACCOUNT_TRUST → external can_assume; CLUSTER_ADMIN on a binding → its bound_to edges). "Breaks" = the path's node sequence no longer survives re-evaluation on that clone; Simulate = full re-traversal on the clone.
- Item 8 (defect 14): `go get golang.org/x/net@v0.60.0` (go.mod line 134; no tidy); `go build ./...` ok; `govulncheck ./...` → "Your code is affected by 0 vulnerabilities" (1 unreachable x/crypto/openpgp advisory remains in a required module). `go get modernc.org/libc@v1.66.3` was a no-op (already required).
- Regression caught in live smoke and fixed: the `import nimbus` probe printed a traceback when the Python layer is absent → probe now silent (`pythonCmd` without stderr).
- Live smoke (binary built to scratchpad, testdata ingested): loopback serve → /api/summary 200, Host rebind 421, cross-site POST 403, same-origin query 200, `SELECT '--';DELETE…` 400 "multiple statements", runaway CTE 408 after 10.0 s, findings count intact (28), /ws with evil Origin 403, / 200. `serve --addr 0.0.0.0:…` without token → refused. Token mode: no token 401, bearer 200, `/?token=` → 303 + cookie → cookie 200, /healthz 200. `serve --help` with the token in env does not print it.
- FINAL: `go vet ./...` ok; `go test ./... -count=1` all ok; `pytest -q` 177 passed; gosec 52 → 54 (+2 G103, the two unsafe.Pointer out-params in store/readonly.go prepareOnce; all other entries are the same findings at shifted lines). Inventory updated: "## Remediated 2026-10-09".

## Track F — one published score (Opus agent)

Scope: internal/risk, new internal/intel, python/nimbus/risk.py + risk_weights.json + tests/test_risk.py (+ tests/fixtures/), docs/scoring.md. No other package was edited.

- **Formula (score_version 2.0.0):** `score = clamp(base × Π multipliers, 0, 10)`, rounded to one decimal.
  - Bands: Critical ≥ 9, High ≥ 7, Medium ≥ 5, Low < 5.
  - Ties are broken by the uncapped raw product.
  - The weights table is in `internal/risk/risk.go` (`DefaultWeights`, `Weights.Table()`) and in `docs/scoring.md` between the `weights:begin/end` markers.
- **Explainability:** every `Ranked` now carries `score_version` and `explanation`.
  - `explanation` holds `score`, `version`, `base`, `base_reason`, `raw`, `factors[{name, multiplier, status, reason, evidence}]`, `top_paths` (≤ 3) and `missing`.
  - `risk.Explain(r)` returns the same data. `cap` and `rounding` factors make `base × Π multipliers` equal the score exactly.
  - An unknown input is `not scored` (×1.0) and is listed in `missing`.
- **Intel:** `internal/intel` `Enricher` loads data in this order: cache (`.cloud-posture/intel`, 24h TTL), then HTTPS fetch, then stale cache, then the embedded snapshot (KEV 2026.10.08 with 200 entries; EPSS 2026-10-09 with 204 rows).
  - `NoNetwork` mode is supported.
  - Fetching uses HTTPS only, keeps TLS verification on, has timeouts and size caps, and validates a download before caching it.
  - Tests use httptest TLS servers and never touch the real network. The snapshot was cut once from the live feeds and saved under `scratchpad/feeds`.
- **Crown jewels:** `risk.JewelFromConfig(cfg)` calls Track C's `config.MatchCrownJewel` (it landed mid-track). Levels map to ×2.0 / 1.6 / 1.2 / 1.0. The fallback is node `crown_jewel` / `criticality`, then `not scored`.
- **Python:** `nimbus.risk` no longer has a formula. It reads Go's ranked JSON from the `risk=` argument, then `NIMBUS_RISK_JSON`, then `risk.json` next to the DB.
  - Findings with no Go score get `risk_score=None` and `band="not scored"`.
  - `--json` echoes only explicit Go output, so the legacy `prioritizeBest` gets `[]` and keeps Go's numbers.
- **Evidence:**
  - Red: the new Go tests failed to build (`undefined: PrioritizeWith/Options/ScoreVersion/Explanation`).
  - First green run: calibration had 4 expectation mismatches. Each matched the hand-computed product (for example `s3_public_marked_public_data` = 5.5 × 1.4 × 0.9 = 6.93, which is Medium). The YAML was corrected to the hand-verified values.
  - Drift probe: changing `ExposureWide` to 1.45 failed `TestWeightsDocumented`, `TestCalibration` (3 scenarios) and `TestPythonGolden`. Reverting it made them pass again.
  - Final runs: `go test ./internal/risk ./internal/intel` passes. `pytest -q` gives 183 passed. `go test ./...` passes except `internal/cli` (build failure in `offboard_test.go`, Track G in flight), `internal/collector/aws` and `internal/remediation`. All three belong to other tracks that are still being edited.
  - Order of work: the intel and Python code were written before their tests, so they have no red step. Only the risk package went test-first.
- **Integration still needed (outside Track F scope):**
  1. `internal/cli/scan.go` `prioritizeBest` must stop merging Python scores (`int(p)/10`). It should simply be `risk.PrioritizeWith(..., risk.Options{Intel: intel.NewEnricher(...).Load(ctx), CrownJewel: risk.JewelFromConfig(cfg)})`, plus a `--no-network` flag.
  2. `internal/cli/report.go` prints "Medium ≥4"; it must say ≥5.
  3. Persist `cloud-posture prioritize --json` to `.cloud-posture/risk.json` so Python pages show Go scores.
  4. `python/nimbus/server.py` renders `None` for unscored rows.
  5. The dashboard (Track I) should show `explanation.factors` and `missing`.

## Phase 0 baseline (Track H ground-truth lab, measured before B/C/F land)
12 labelled paths: 8 found at any score, 2 at ≥7; precision 26.7% (12 of 45 reported); seeded false positive (trusted CIDR) wrongly reported with 13 paths; no unlabelled path ≥7.
Root causes → owners: cluster-role-binding classed compute + placeholder node keeps guessed type (B); scores too low for textbook chains (F + B scoring inputs); trusted_cidrs not applied to ingested findings (B/C); SCP deny not evaluated (Phase 2 IAM, pending-feature); user-as-entry-and-target never yields a path (B); **native ingest cannot carry typed relationships** (model/adapters/graph — new Track B2 after B).
Noise source: rule chains joined only by "same account" (B: require a real edge, not co-tenancy).

## Integration backlog (for the post-track integration pass; owner: internal/cli + internal/server wiring agent)
- F: `prioritizeBest` → `risk.PrioritizeWith(Options{Intel, CrownJewel})`; `--no-network`; write `.cloud-posture/risk.json`; fix "Medium ≥4" banner → ≥5; Python server prints None for unscored.

- G: `/api/summary` from `IssueCounts()`; coverage view in API.

- New Track B2: typed relationships in native ingest (`related: [{id, kind}]`), placeholder node typing, user-as-entry-and-target, require a real edge (not same-account) to chain rules, apply trusted exposure to ingested findings.

## Verification of security hardening (Opus verifier)
Independent, read-only. Binary built from source at 18:27 (`cp`) and rebuilt at 18:42 (`cp2`, after a concurrent runner.go change). Live `serve` on 127.0.0.1:18731 (no token) / :18732 (`--token`); Python `nimbus serve` on :18741 (no token) / :18742 (`CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN`). Scratch: `scratchpad/verify-a/` (`sqlprobe.py`, `exp/main.go`, `pt/`, `mal/`, `scope/`, `d3/`).

| Defect | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Go `/api/query` | **PARTIAL**: injection/write/file bypasses closed; a new DoS is open | 54 payloads, 0 writes, 0 files created. `SELECT '--';DELETE` → 400 "multiple statements"; CTE+DELETE and WITH…INSERT…RETURNING → 400 "not read-only"; ATTACH/PRAGMA/VACUUM INTO/REINDEX/ANALYZE/SAVEPOINT/EXPLAIN DELETE → 400; sqlite_master, temp.sqlite_master, `"sqlite_schema"`, `main.[sqlite_schema]`, json_each over sqlite_schema, PRAGMA_TABLE_INFO, homoglyphs → 400; load_extension → "not authorized"; writefile/readfile absent. Runaway recursive CTE → **408 in 3.00 s** (`--query-timeout 3s`). Trailing `;` only and `/*;*/` → 200 (correct). Gaps: see "New findings" N1 and N4. |
| 1 Python `/api/query` | **PARTIAL** | Same bypass set: all 400, no writes. Runaway CTE → 400 "exceeded the 10s timeout" in 10.2 s (400, not 408). `printf('%.*c',1e9,'x')` ran **55.7 s** and `randomblob(5e8)` ran 13.3 s, both past the 10 s limit (N2). ### New findings
- **N1 (MEDIUM, DoS, Go): `SELECT length(randomblob(1000000000))` wedges every `/api/query` for minutes.** A single request returned 408 after 13.3 s, not 3 s. With 3 concurrent requests, all later `/api/query` calls (even `SELECT 1`) hung for over 60 s while `/api/schema` still answered. The goroutine dump (SIGQUIT) shows the worker inside `Xsqlite3_randomness` → `_chacha_block` holding SQLite's process-global PRNG mutex, and the other handlers blocked in `Xsqlite3_open_v2 → _sqlite3Fts5Init → Xsqlite3_randomness → mutexEnter` at `readonly.go:125` (`db.Conn`). `sqlite3_interrupt` cannot stop a single function call. This needs the token, or loopback access when no token is set; cross-site requests are blocked. **Fix: call `sqlite.Limit(conn, sqlite3.SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH, 1<<20)` (public modernc API) on the read-only conn.** In the experiment this rejects randomblob(1e9) in 0.00 s ("string or blob too big"). The same cap covers zeroblob/printf/replace amplification.
- **N2 (LOW-MEDIUM, DoS, Python):** the progress handler does not fire inside one function call. `printf('%.*c',1e9,'x')` took 55.7 s and allocated about 1 GB. Fix: `con.setlimit(sqlite3.SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH, 1<<20)` (Python 3.11+).
- **N3 (LOW, integrity): scan IDs have one-second granularity.** Two stored scans in the same second share an ID: the `scans` row is overwritten, and the second (clean) scan's `--fail-on LOW` exited 2 on the first scan's findings. Reproduced twice. Fix: add sub-second precision or a random suffix in `newScanID()`.
- **N4 (LOW, info): the `dbstat` virtual table is readable** (200, lists `sqlite_schema` pages and sizes). The EXPLAIN check only inspects OpenRead and does not check `VOpen`. Contents are not exposed. Fix: allowlist VOpen on json_each/json_tree only.
- **N5 (LOW, functional, Python):** `WITH RECURSIVE c(x) AS (… LIMIT 10) SELECT count(*) FROM c` → 400 "not allowed: read c". The authorizer denies READ on CTE names when the count optimisation reads them.
- Pre-existing, not from this track: SARIF `"results": null` on a clean scan (also with `--no-store`), but SARIF 2.1.0 requires an array, so code-scanning upload may reject it. `alert` exits 0 when every delivery fails (defect 20). The Python server has no Host check, so `GET /api/findings` with `Host: evil.example` → 200 even with CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN set (DNS-rebinding read). Its cross-site text/plain POST got 422.

### readonly.go assessment
- **Fail-safe:** `sqliteHandle` fails closed (refuses every query) if the driver's conn is not a struct, has no `db` field, the field is not a uintptr, or it is 0. It does **not** fail safe if a future modernc keeps a uintptr `db` field with different meaning: `prepare_v2` and `interrupt` would get a bad pointer and could crash the process. That is mitigated by the go.sum pin (v1.39.0). Recommend a runtime self-check after `sqliteHandle`, e.g. require `Xsqlite3_db_filename(tls, h, "main")` to equal the store path, else refuse. Also consider allocating the out-params with `libc.Xmalloc` instead of the Go global + mutex. That removes the G103s and the process-wide serialisation.
- **Driver experiment (`verify-a/exp/main.go`, modernc v1.39.0):**
  - (A) A 1 s context on a runaway inside the first step returns "deadline exceeded" in 1.03 s.
  - (B) A runaway inside a later `rows.Next` step is **still running after 8 s**. Driver context cancellation covers only the first step, so the explicit `sqlite3_interrupt` is justified.
  - (C) `mode=ro&_pragma=query_only(1)` blocks DELETE, CREATE TEMP and writes to attached DBs.
  - (C) It does **not** block `ATTACH 'new.db'`, which succeeds and creates a 0-byte file, or `ATTACH` of an existing DB, which succeeds and makes `SELECT … FROM o.secret` return data from the other DB.
  - (C) `VACUUM INTO` errors but **still creates a 0-byte file**.
  - (C) `PRAGMA query_only=0` succeeds, but writes stay blocked by mode=ro.
  - So the DSN alone is **not** equally strong. Arbitrary SQLite files on disk become readable through ATTACH. The code comment "ATTACH cannot create files" is wrong, although the lexer and prepare layers do stop ATTACH and VACUUM in practice.
- **Alternatives:**
  - modernc v1.39.0 exposes no Go authorizer or progress-handler API. `Xsqlite3_set_authorizer` exists but needs a C-callable function pointer, which means more unsafe code, not less.
  - `sqlite.Limit` is the only relevant supported hook and should be added (N1).
  - **Recommendation:** keep the layered design and add LIMIT_LENGTH, the handle self-check, and VOpen allowlisting.
  - Longer term, a driver with a supported authorizer, progress handler and interrupt (ncruces/go-sqlite3, or mattn with cgo) would remove the reflection and unsafe code.

## Integration pass 1 — CLI/API wiring (Opus agent)

Scope: internal/cli, internal/server, internal/output and their tests. **One scope exception:** `internal/dashboard/render.go` gained three additive `Data` fields (`issues`, `coverage`, `scans`) plus the `store` import. Item 5 cannot be done without them, and the dashboard HTML/JS was not touched. Git was not touched and `go mod tidy` was not run. `github.com/spf13/pflag` (already in go.mod as indirect) is now imported directly by `cli_test.go`.

**Red evidence**
- New tests: `internal/cli/integration_test.go` (642 lines), `internal/server/api_issues_test.go`, `internal/output/output_test.go`.
- CLI was compile-red (`vet: undefined: intelHook` …).
- Server was red:
  - `issues: []` and `coverage: []` (routes missing; they fell through to `GET /`);
  - `include_resolved: current 2, all 2`;
  - "a whitespace-only token must not unlock a non-loopback bind";
  - summary open/resolved did not come from IssueCounts.
- Output was red: `results = null, want []` and `rules = null, want []`.
- After green, I mutated the implementation to prove the tests bite. Each mutation was reverted from the scratchpad backup afterwards. Results:
  - scan id back to second precision → "duplicate scan id 20261009T120000Z after 1 ids".
  - Alert error removed → "alert exited 0 although every delivery failed".
  - risk.json write disabled → "ingest did not write …/risk.json".
  - AccountTimeout/TrustedCIDRs dropped → "trusted_cidrs not passed … []" and "deadline in 9m59.99s".
  - `NoNetwork` forced false → "--no-network made 2 outbound connection(s)". The first version of this test missed it because keep-alive reused the connection; it now counts requests as well as connections.
  - Typed jewels removed from traversal → "very_high crown jewel must be a tier-0 traversal target". The first version missed this because `customer-pii-exports` matches the built-in `*pii*` rule; it now uses `ledger-store`.
  - Unescaped ID pattern → "a node no rule matches must not become a tier-0 target" (`ledger-store-2`).
- Green on first run (guards, no code change needed): `TestConfigValidationErrorsSurface` (the loader already calls Validate; scan, serve and doctor exit 1 with `config: integrations.slack.token: literal secret values are not allowed…`, and the value is not printed) and `TestNewRoutesBehindGuards`.
- Test-harness fixes:
  - `run()`/`runErr()` now drain the stdout pipe concurrently. Large JSON output deadlocked the old helper (a 40 s hang in `paths --format json --fixes`).
  - `resetFlags()` resets the cobra flags each run changed.
  - `TestMain` sets `CLOUD_POSTURE_NO_NETWORK=1`, so no cli test reaches the real feeds.

**Per item**
1. **F, one score.**
   - `prioritizeBest` → `rankAll` = `risk.PrioritizeWith(fs, g, paths, riskOptions(ctx,cfg))` (scan.go:525–537). The Python merge (`int(p)/10`) is deleted. The `--no-store` path uses the same function.
   - Global `--no-network` flag and `CLOUD_POSTURE_NO_NETWORK=1` (root.go:69, 82).
   - Intel feed is loaded once per process; its cache lives beside the DB (`<dbdir>/intel`) (root.go:111).
   - `<dbdir>/risk.json` holds the full ranking, written atomically after scan/ingest (finishScanWith) and after `prioritize` (scan.go:547).
   - `prioritize --explain` prints the factor table (report.go:306). The banner now reads "Critical ≥9, High ≥7, Medium ≥5, Low <5" with the score_version (report.go:289).
   - `/api/risk/top` uses the same options through `Server.RiskOptions` (server.go:55, serve.go:71).
   - Python `load_go_risk('.cloud-posture/risk.json')` → 25 entries, score_version 2.0.0.
2. **G, Issues/coverage API (server.go).**
   - `/api/summary`: top-level counts come from `IssueCounts()` (`unit:"issues"`, total/open/resolved/combinations/by_*). The legacy finding counts are under `findings`; `attack_paths` and `critical_paths` stay at top level.
   - `GET /api/issues` filters: `state` = open|reopened|resolved|all (400 otherwise), `severity`, `min_severity`, `provider`, `account`, `kind` (400 otherwise), `limit`.
   - `GET /api/issues/{id}` returns `{issue, findings}`, with resolved evidence included; unknown id → 404.
   - `GET /api/coverage?scan_id=` (default: latest scan).
   - `GET /api/scans?limit=` returns history newest first with by_severity (`server.ScanHistory`).
   - `GET /api/findings?include_resolved=1`.
   - All of these sit behind the existing Host/token/CSRF middleware (tested: 401, 421, cross-site POST 403).
3. **Verifier items.**
   - N3 scan id = `20060102T150405.000Z-<16 hex random>` (root.go:415). Tested with 2000 ids under a frozen clock, two same-second stored scans (2 rows, gate 0), and child-process back-to-back scans (exit 2, then exit 0, 2 distinct rows).
   - A whitespace-only or empty `--token`, or a whitespace `CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN`, is refused with a clear error (serve.go:102). `server.ValidateBind` trims the token.
   - SARIF on a clean scan has `results: []` and `rules: []` (output.go:134).
   - `alert` prints a `per sink:` summary and exits non-zero when deliveries were attempted and every one failed (report.go:204).
   - New `scan|ingest --diff-json FILE|-`, with counts plus details of new/resolved/reopened (lifecycle.go:146–190).
4. **UX.**
   - `cli.SetVersion(v, c, d)`; `version` prints version, commit, build date and score version. **E's main.go still sets `cli.Version = buildVersion()`; switch it to `cli.SetVersion(version, commit, date)`** (left to E).
   - `paths` flags:
     - `--explain`: per-hop `because:`.
     - Default: a "Dead ends: N" line.
     - `--dead-ends`: list, or `{dead_ends, findings}` JSON.
     - `--no-dead-ends`: hides the line and clears dead_ends from `--fixes` JSON. It cannot be combined with `--dead-ends`.
     - `--choke <node id|name>`: paths through the node, "closes N", and path ids (JSON too). An unknown node is an error.
     - `--fixes` now shows closes N and `min_fix_exact` / `min_fix_verified`.
   - New `plan [--json] [--top 5]` (plan.go): MinFixSet with `min_fix_exact` / `min_fix_verified`. For the top 5 fixes it shows `SimulateWith` "resolves N / remaining score", alone and cumulative. The test re-simulates step 1 and matches.
5. **Dashboard payload.** `renderDashboard` passes `issues`, `coverage` and `scans` (loaded in finishScanWith/loadResult via `loadLifecycleViews`). `risk[]` entries carry `explanation`. The test parses the embedded data blob.
6. **C, collector options.** `collectorOptions(cfg)` passes `Concurrency`, `AccountTimeout: cfg.AccountTimeout()` and `TrustedCIDRs` (scan.go:104). The test uses a fake collector through `collectorHook`, checking the 7 s deadline and the CIDR.
7. **C, crown jewels.**
   - The raw `scan.crown_jewels` parse (`crownJewels()`) is deleted.
   - `traversalOptions(cfg, g)` turns typed `cfg.CrownJewels` into exact-ID traversal targets via `risk.JewelFromConfig` (very_high 4, high 3, medium 2, low 1). Every byte is escaped, so neither `path.Match` nor the substring fallback in `attackpath.Criticality` can match another node.
   - The risk explanation shows "crown_jewels rule <name>".
   - **README still documents `scan.crown_jewels` (README.md:133, owned by E)**.
8. **C, validation.** scan, serve and doctor surface the typed `config:` errors and exit 1. Guard test only; no code change needed.

**Final runs (19:0x)**

- `go test ./internal/cli/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/output/ -count=1`: all ok.
- `go test ./... -count=1`: 30 packages ok, 0 failures.

## Planning decision (main session) — toxic-combination floor
B2 result: precision 26.7% → 89.5%, recall 8/12 → 10/12 (12/12 projected with typed lab), recall@7 6/12. Six found paths sit under 7 because no exploit evidence lifts the per-hop likelihood. Decision: adopt an explicit, published rule mirroring Wiz toxic combinations / Tenable toxic trilogy — wide internet exposure + admin-level or sensitive-data target ⇒ path likelihood floor 0.7, tagged `toxic_combination`, never for narrow/trusted exposure or weak-link paths. P07 (public EKS endpoint alone) relabelled Medium (needs a credential; P08 is the credential chain). Track B3 launched: typed lab generator, CLI trusted-CIDR wiring, floor rule, known-gaps prune, final numbers.
`TestQueryEndpointBusyIs429` re-run 5× alone after the view-lock fix: 5/5 ok (B2's failure predated the fix).

   - Weaknesses come from `TOXIC` (clients.js:155), a mirror of `model.ToxicCombinations` that a Go test keeps in step.
2. **Coverage honesty (check 18).** `vCoverage` (clients.js:1396):
   - One row per provider/account with OK/DENIED/SKIPPED/ERROR/TIMEOUT columns. Denied, timeout and error are red3; skipped is grey; ok is green.
   - A "Not covered" list gives the API and reason for each entry.
   - Tool runs stay below. If `coverage` is empty, the old `scan.tools` view is used (tested).
   - The overview coverage panel shows status counts that link to `#coverage`.
3. **Trend (check 19).** `trendBlock` (clients.js:562) draws one sparkline per severity from `scans`, oldest to newest, up to 24 cells, all on one shared scale, in panel foreground colour only. It also shows the current count and the change since the previous scan.
   - **The payload lacked lifecycle counts:** `scans` rows are `model.ScanSummary` (total, failed, by_severity, by_provider, attack_paths, tools) with no new/resolved. The panel says "scan history carries totals only (no new/resolved counts per scan)".
   - If a row gains `new`/`resolved`/`reopened` (or a `lifecycle` object), it prints "N new / M resolved since last scan" (tested with a modified payload).
   - Also: `scans.by_severity` counts open **findings**, not Issues, and is labelled as such.
4. **Explain (check 20).** `explainBlock` (clients.js:1122) prints:
   - `base 7.0 — reason`;
   - each scored factor as `name ×1.40 — reason`, with a hanging indent and grey evidence under it;
   - `missing`, holding the not-scored factors plus `explanation.missing`, de-duplicated;
   - `top paths`, which are clickable.

   `scoreFooter` puts `score version 2.0.0` as the pane's last line. The Issue pane explains its highest-scored evidence finding.
5. **Paths (check 21).** `vPaths` (clients.js:687) and `pathSteps` (clients.js:677):
   - A grey `because:` line under every hop, word-wrapped and indented. It appears in the paths panel, and the finding and Issue panes print the top path hop by hop.
   - The header reads `13 attack paths · dead ends: 8 [show dead ends]`. The toggle lists the dead-end resources with their open-Issue counts.
   - In the findings section, dead-end Issues (from `reaches_crown_jewel`) are hidden by default and the count line says how many. Every drill-through from overview or compliance passes `deadends=1`, so its numbers match.
   - Choke points: clicking one shows only its `path_ids` and the line "fixing it closes N paths" (`#paths?choke=`). Overview choke rows now drill to this view, and a CLOSES column was added.
   - The min-fix-set heading shows `exact`/`greedy` and `verified by re-traversal`/`unverified`.
   - `N incomplete` is shown in grey and is not counted in the header.
   - The `suppressed (trusted)` section and grey `weak_link` rows are tested with a modified payload. The real testdata has `suppressed: null` and no weak_link paths.
6. **Re-layout (check 22).**
   - `mount()` attaches a ResizeObserver per live view (clients.js:262). When the panel's width in character cells changes, it redraws the view (debounced 40 ms) and calls `CWM.applyFocus` (cwm.js:465). The observer is disconnected in onClose.
   - `vFindings` recomputes the width on every apply.
   - `fitCols` (clients.js:96) drops optional columns by priority so the title column keeps at least 28–30 cells.
   - The check asserts header ≤ panel width with the pane open, after it closes (the header must widen again), and at a 1100px viewport.
7. **Live (checks 15, 23).** cwm.js:420 also fetches `/api/issues?state=all`; if it fails, only that piece is null, so an older server still works. `issueCounts` (clients.js:186) takes the counts from `/api/summary` with `unit:"issues"` or from a nested `issues` object. With the legacy shape it counts from the Issue list. All three shapes are tested.

**Look:** no new colours. Check 3 (the style audit of every section, including reverse video in check 13) stays green. CSS added: `.act`, `.gone`, `.wrap`, `.because`.

**Final:**
- `go vet ./internal/dashboard/` ok.
- `go test ./internal/dashboard/ -count=1` → ok (10 tests).
- `make ui-test` → 24/24 PASS, "all checks passed".

- Item 4 RED (`go test ./tests/groundtruth -run Integrity` after adding P07 `band: medium, min_score: 5, max_score: 7, band_reason`): `field band / max_score / band_reason not found in type groundtruth.gtPath`; new tests/groundtruth/band_test.go `TestBandOutcome` was compile-red only (written together with `expectedOutcome`/`inBand`; not observed failing on behaviour). GREEN: gtPath `MaxScore/Band/BandReason`, `inBand`, `expectedOutcome` (new failure kind `above_band:<id>`), integrity allows a sub-threshold expected label only with band + band_reason + max_score ≤ threshold; report splits `recall@7 (best ≥ 7)` and `recall in band`. P07 → 5.15 FOUND in band (PUBLIC_K8S_API_TAKEOVER rule chain). P08 ≥ 7 verified through the CLI on typed relations: 6.86 → 7.00 by the toxic floor (exposed+admin, target cluster-admin binding / ClusterRole).
- Item 5: lab/known_gaps.yaml pruned to 2 entries: `low_score:P12` (owner planning, see deviation above) and `pending_feature_reported:S01` (Phase 2). `make groundtruth` PASS (2 known gaps); `GROUNDTRUTH_STRICT=1 make groundtruth` FAIL with exactly `[strict] low_score:P12` (3.80 < 7) and `[strict] pending_feature_reported:S01`. docs/benchmark.md: "Current numbers (after Track B3)" table (baseline / B2 / now), new "Toxic-combination floor" subsection, generator section marked done, band matching rule; docs/scoring.md "Path score" gained the floor bullet.
- FINAL numbers (`make groundtruth`): reported 21 paths (13 ≥ 7); recall 100% (12/12); recall@7 83.3% (10/12; P07 is medium by label, P12 3.80); recall in band 91.7% (11/12); precision 95.2% (20/21; the extra is `internet > nimbus-lab-eks` 4.00); precision@7 100% (13/13); unlabelled ≥ 7: 0; F01 0 paths + 1 suppressed; S01 2 paths (7.60).
- End: `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` 31 packages ok, 0 failures (CLOUD_POSTURE_NO_NETWORK=1). gofmt clean on touched files.

## Track I accepted (main session review of v3-overview.png, v3-finding-explain.png)
Issues-first overview (26 issues, 3 toxic), trend sparklines, coverage panel, explain factors + per-hop because incl. the toxic-combination reason in the pane; look unchanged (cwm v2). ui-smoke 24/24. Payload gap noted: scans history lacks per-scan new/resolved counts (Phase 1 item: persist lifecycle counts per scan).

## Planning decision — P12 (unprotected repo → OIDC wildcard trust → admin deploy role)
Real-world critical (pipeline poisoning), but the toxic floor is defined on internet exposure. Decision: Phase 1 adds a second, documented floor class "CI entry": unprotected default branch (or any-contributor write) + OIDC/role trust with wildcard/unpinned subject ⇒ likelihood floor 0.7 to admin-level targets. Lives with the plan-time path diff work (same CI/CD posture area). P12 remains a listed known gap until then; S01 awaits Phase 2 SCP evaluation.

## Phase 0 gate (main session, after B3 + I)
`go vet` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` → 31 packages ok, exit 0; pytest 222 passed; `make ui-test` all checks (24) + no page errors; `make groundtruth` PASS with 2 known gaps (P12 planning, S01 pending SCP); strict mode fails on exactly those 2; govulncheck: 0 vulnerabilities; `gofmt -l` lists 10 pre-existing unformatted files (hygiene item). Verifier 2 still running.

## Council (Bobby: "get the council ... https://github.com/karpathy/llm-council with all fable agents")
Pattern: stage 1 — 4 independent Fable members (lenses: CISO/RFP buyer, CNAPP product architect, OSS distribution strategist, attack-path/red-team skeptic) answer the same question; stage 2 — each ranks the anonymised answers of the others; stage 3 — chairman (main session) synthesises → docs/council/decision.md and plan v2 amendments. Outputs in docs/council/.

| item | verdict | evidence |

| 15a AWS NotAction | PARTIAL | `go test ./internal/collector/aws -run ZZVerifyIsAdmin -v`: 8 of 14 adversarial cases are wrong. `NotAction ["iam:*"]` is not admin (reasonable). Wrong: `Action iam:*` on `*` is not admin; `Resource arn:*` is not broad; any Condition suppresses the verdict; Allow* + Deny* is still admin; `NotResource *` is broad; lower-case `allow` is accepted (policy.go:143-186). |
| 15b S3 account BPA | VERIFIED | 3-of-4 account flags, account IgnorePublicAcls-only, bucket+account flags combining, and 403 on s3control (recorded as unknown plus CollectError, not a pass) all correct. |

A port range is judged by its lowest port only (nsg.go:274 `lowPort`): allow 20-30 with deny 20 first → not exposed. A referenced NSG that was not collected is treated as deny (nsg.go:223). |
| 15d GCP | PARTIAL | Tie → deny wins; deny 0.0.0.0/0 at 65535 vs allow at 1000 → exposed; disabled, EGRESS and tags correct. Explicit priority 0 is treated as 1000 (gcp.go:553-558; the author recorded this as a gap) → false positive and false negative. Port ranges use the lowest port (gcp.go:688-693). |

| 20 account timeout | PARTIAL | A collector that blocks forever is abandoned at timeout+grace (50+50 ms → 100.7 ms; default grace measured 30.10 s); its graph does not leak. Gaps: a collector that honours ctx but returns partial results with a nil error after the deadline gets status `ok` with no timeout error (collector.go:208). On parent cancel, a stuck collector is abandoned silently as `ok` (collector.go:191-213). |

### Integration pass 1
| item | verdict | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `prioritize --explain` factors multiply to the score | VERIFIED | `prioritize --json --top 0` over 25 entries: base × Π(multipliers excl. cap/rounding) = `raw` exactly, and × cap/rounding = `risk_score` in all 25. Bands are consistent with ≥9/≥7/≥5. |

Defect: the path is per directory, not per DB (scan.go:543). A second DB in the same dir overwrote it with `[]`, and Python then reports the first DB as `risk_score None, band "not scored"`. |
| `/api/issues` filters | PARTIAL | state=bogus → 400; kind=nope → 400; state/kind/severity/provider/limit work. `min_severity=zzz` → 200 with all 26 (filter silently dropped); `severity=bogus` → 200 []; `limit=-1/abc/1e20` → ignored. |
| `/api/summary` Issue counts | VERIFIED | See IssueCounts above. |

| whitespace token | VERIFIED + note | `--token "   "`, tab, `""` and `CLOUD_POSTURE_TOKEN="  "` are all refused with a clear error. Note: `--token " abc "` is accepted at startup but no request can match it (`Bearer abc` → 401), so the operator is locked out. |
| SARIF `results: []` | VERIFIED | Clean IaC scan: `-o sarif` → `results [] rules []`; the `--sarif` file → `[]`. |
| `alert` exit code | VERIFIED + note | Slack+Teams to a dead port → per-sink summary, "every delivery failed (42 of 42)", exit 1. Failed deliveries are retried on the next run. Note: when one sink fails completely and another succeeds, the exit code is 0. |
| `plan` "resolves N" = re-simulation | VERIFIED | Independent end-to-end re-simulation: stored finding docs exported as native JSON (round-trip baseline 9 paths / 52.67 = plan), each step's finding_ids flipped to PASS alone and cumulatively, then re-ingested into a fresh DB and `plan --json` re-run. 18/18 match resolves and remaining score. (Editing `findings` rows in place does not change `plan`; it reads the stored graph.) |
| `paths --choke`, `--dead-ends` | PARTIAL | `--choke web-1` and the ARN form → 1 path, closes 1, path id matches. JSON has `choke_point.path_ids`. An unknown node → exit 1. `--dead-ends` + `--no-dead-ends` → exit 1. Issues: (a) the choke count covers traversal paths only. Rule-chain path f79767f9 also runs through web-1 but is not counted, while `paths` lists both. Non-choke nodes (`payments-prod`, on 2 paths) are refused as "not a choke point". (b) The dead-end list includes `sg-0abc123` (open SSH on web-1). The finding hint links web-1 → the bare id `sg-0abc123`, while the SG node is the full ARN, so the SG never joins web-1's path (graph id resolution, B2/B3 area). |

### New defects (severity, location, repro)
1. HIGH — D1: a tfstate-mapped finding for one module patches another module's file. `internal/iac/iac.go:221-225,251-254`: `byAddr` is keyed by `type.name`, the module is dropped, and the last file parsed wins. Repro: `scratchpad/verify-2/d/modrepro`, `cp --db m.db fix --finding EC2_IMDSV1 --patch --iac infra --tfstate state.json --print` → "mapped i-aaa → modules/b/main.tf:1" (i-aaa is in module.a). `verifyPatch` (cli/fix.go:320-338) compares stripped addresses and does not catch it.
2. MEDIUM-HIGH — D3: `scan --iac` dies with `fatal error: stack overflow` (exit 2, not recoverable) on 70k nested `[` (140 KB .tf). Repro: `scratchpad/verify-2/so/deep.tf`. A malicious PR kills the hook or Action. Fix: cap nesting depth or size before hclsyntax.
3. MEDIUM — lifecycle scopes come only from observed findings (`internal/cli/lifecycle.go:35` → `store.ScopesOf`). When the last resource of a source/provider/account/root disappears (e.g. the public bucket block is deleted), its findings stay open forever. Repro: scratchpad/verify-2/i/last: bucket-only main.tf → 1 open; replaced by `variable "x" {}` → "0 resolved", state open. The same thing happens with a native collector that returns no findings (mode=none in the G probe). Contrast: if a PASS row of the same scope remains, the deletion resolves. Related: `ingest` of a valid empty `[]` errors "no findings parsed".
4. MEDIUM — offboard rollback (`internal/cli/offboard.go` rollback, ~l.330-395) has no check that the manifest account equals the current STS account and no allowlist of onboard names or `managed-by` tags. Replaying account 111's manifest with account 222's credentials detached SecurityAudit from 222's own CloudPostureScanRole before failing. A manifest naming `OrganizationAccountAccessRole` deleted it ("✓ rollback verified").
5. MEDIUM — D2: a duplicate `yor_trace` is missed when tags use `merge()` (iac/patch.go:330-352), so the wrong block is patched instead of being refused.
6. MEDIUM — D4: kubectl/az option injection through a resource name starting with `-` (`remediation/generator.go:218` shellSafe; :377, :388; no `--`). Example: `--server=https://evil` sends the kubeconfig credentials there. Requires hostile ingested data.
7. MEDIUM — Python `ensure_views` reverts the Go resolved-exclusion views (store.py:433-434, inventory columns) on every Python ingest (A2 scope).
8. MEDIUM — the collector false PASS/positives listed in Track C (M365 OR operator and ignored conditions, Okta risk/IdP conditions, GCP priority 0, port ranges by lowest port, IsAdmin gaps), the cross-host Authorization header leak on pagination (core/rest.go:93-125), and unchecked secret-bearing config fields (config/validate.go:293-312).
9. LOW-MEDIUM — D5: patching memory grows with the square of file size (iac.go:151 UnifiedDiff LCS): 12k lines → 1.33 GB, and 10 MB → OOM. D6: a `dynamic "metadata_options"` gets a duplicate static block added; dynamic ingress with a literal 0.0.0.0/0 → PASS; NSG `source_address_prefixes=["*"]` → PASS (scan.go:280,322,424; patch.go:621-626).
10. LOW-MEDIUM — risk.json is per directory, not per DB (cli/scan.go:543).

## Fix track V2-iac (Opus agent)
Scope: internal/iac/** and internal/remediation/** only (no git, no `go mod tidy`). Covers Verification 2 defects 1 (D1), 2 (D3), 5 (D2), 6 (D4), 9 (D5). Tests come from the verifier probes (`scratchpad/verify-2/tree/internal/{iac,remediation}/zz_verify_*`); those probes only logged results, so they were rewritten as assertions under proper names. Every test was run red before the fix.

| defect | test (file) | red (before the fix) | green | fix (file:line) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 HIGH: cross-module tfstate mapping | `TestMapCloudToCodeResolvesModuleAddress`, `TestPatchRefusesModuleAddressInOtherModuleFile`, `TestMapCloudToCodeRefusesNonUniqueAddress`, `TestMapCloudToCodeShowJSONChildModules` (iac/module_map_test.go) | i-aaa, i-bbb and i-root all mapped to `modules/b/main.tf` with addr `aws_instance.web`. A data source was mapped. `module.b…` with file modules/a or main.tf was patched. A duplicate address was located at two.tf:1. `show -json` child modules were not mapped. | PASS | iac.go:248-597. `CodeLocation` now carries the full address (`module.b.aws_instance.web[0]`, using index_key), `Unresolved` and `Candidates`. `MapCloudToCode` resolves each module path through local `module` `source`s from root (`moduleIndex.resolve` :525, `locate` :558) and looks for type.name only in that module's directory. Data sources are skipped. `values.root_module.child_modules` are walked. A cloud ID or yor_trace that is shared by different resources is marked unresolved. `Locate` returns false, and the new `LocateStrict` (:398) returns a `TargetError` (errors.Is `ErrAmbiguousTarget`) when the address is not unique or has no file. patch.go:98 makes `PatchTerraform` refuse a module address whose module directory is not the finding's file directory. CLI repro `scratchpad/verify-2/d/modrepro` (binary rebuilt): "mapped i-aaa → modules/a/main.tf:1 (module.a.aws_instance.web)", and the diff touches only modules/a/main.tf. |
| 2 MED-HIGH: stack overflow on deep HCL | `TestScanHCLPathologicalNestingIsAnErrorFinding` (10 shapes: 70k `[`, 300k `(`, 200k calls, objects, `"${`, 1M `!`, 1M `-`, 400k `?:`, ternary lines inside parens, 300k nested blocks), `TestScanTerraformPathologicalFileDoesNotStopTheScan`, `TestReasonableNestingStillParses`, `TestFileSizeLimitIsConfigurable` (iac/limits_test.go) | `runtime: goroutine stack exceeds 1000000000-byte limit / fatal error: stack overflow` (test binary died). Probe of the constructs: `[ ( { f( "${ for` overflow at 100k levels; `! - ?: blocks` overflow at 600k; `+` chains and traversals do not. | PASS. Verifier nasty probe re-run: nested_tuple/paren/not_100k and nested50000_sg each give 1 ERROR finding in 11-34 ms. big10MB gives an ERROR finding (size) in <1 ms. | New limits.go. `checkParseLimits` (:83) runs before every parse (`parseConfig` :169; used by scan.go `parseHCL`, patch.go, symbols.go). Defaults: 2 MiB (`CLOUD_POSTURE_IAC_MAX_FILE_BYTES` or `SetLimits`) and depth 256. Depth is computed over `hclsyntax.LexConfig` tokens (the lexer is not recursive, :100). It counts brackets, braces, parens, quote/heredoc/`${`/`%{`, `%{if/for}` directives, runs of unary `!`/`-`, and chained `?` (reset at `,` and at newlines in `{}` bodies). scan.go:32 skips oversize files using stat, before reading them. scan.go:541 `limitFindings` emits one ERROR finding per file (`IAC_PARSE_LIMIT`), and `ScanHCL` also returns an error. `FuzzScanHCL` seeds now include the 70k-`[` file and 300k `!` (`addPathologicalSeeds`). |
| 5 MED: duplicate yor_trace inside merge() | `TestYorTraceDuplicateInsideMergeIsAmbiguous`, `TestYorTraceNestedExpressionsStillResolve` (iac/trace_dup_test.go) | `aws_instance.y` was patched. A unique trace inside `merge()` gave "no block matches". | PASS. Probe b2 → "2 blocks match (candidates: aws_instance.x, aws_instance.y)". | patch.go:364 `blockTraces` walks the whole `tags` expression (`hclsyntax.VisitAll`) for `yor_trace = "<lit>"`. :390 `mentionsLiteral` makes any block containing the trace literal a candidate. :450 refuses more than one candidate, a candidate whose tags do not set the trace, and a block with conflicting traces. |
| 6 MED: kubectl/az option injection | `TestGeneratedCommandsResistOptionInjection` (names `-n evil`, `--namespace=x`, `--server=https://evil.example`, `-n=kube-system`, `--kubeconfig=…`, `-oyaml` as name, namespace, kind, resource group and canonical ID; every command runs through bash with argv-printing stubs), `TestKubectlArgvBindsHostileValues` (exact argv) (remediation/shell_option_test.go) | e.g. `kubectl patch deployment --namespace=x -n default …` and `az … --resource-group '-n evil'`: the hostile value was a separate argv element in option position. | PASS. The existing quoting test `TestApplyCommandsQuoteResourceNames` still passes. | generator.go:364-395. Every flag value is bound with `=` (`--namespace=`, `--type=`, `--patch-file=`, `--filename=`, `--resource-group=`, `--template-file=`, `--stack-name=`). kubectl kind and name follow `--`. All values and file names are `shq`-quoted. I chose `--` plus `=` binding over refusing names, so the existing metacharacter-quoting contract still holds. |
| 9 LOW-MED: patch memory quadratic | `TestPatchLargeFileIsLinear`, `TestUnifiedDiffWorstCaseIsBounded`, `TestUnifiedDiffSmallUnchanged` (output characterisation) (iac/limits_test.go) | 2 MiB (123k lines): **47.2 s, 119,749 MB allocated**. 60k-line all-different diff: 27,683 MB. | 2 MiB: **0.46 s, live-heap peak 146 MB (GC target 10%), 740 MB total allocated**. 1 MiB: 0.23 s / 74 MB / 375 MB. That is linear: 2x input gives 2x time and allocation. The worst-case diff is bounded. | iac.go:150-240 `UnifiedDiff` trims the common prefix and suffix, then runs an int32 LCS only on the changed middle, capped at 4M cells (16 MB); beyond the cap it emits a plain -/+ block. Output is streamed into a strings.Builder. patch.go:164-250 `patchSource`: hclsyntax parses the file once to find the target, then hclwrite parses, fixes and formats **only the target block's bytes**, which are spliced back. The old code parsed the whole file with hclwrite, ran Format on the whole file and parsed it twice more. Validation re-parses only the changed block and the appended variables, and nothing outside the target is reformatted. Note: the peak is measured as live heap. Under default GOGC=100 the heap peak is ~228 MB at 2 MiB. The floor is the HCL lexer: `hclsyntax.ParseConfig` of the same file alone peaks at 146 MB live / 212 MB at GOGC=100. Total allocation <200 MB is not reachable while hclsyntax lexes 2 MiB (one lex allocates ~298 MB). |

Gates (19:5x, live tree):
- `go vet ./...` → exit 0
- `go test ./internal/iac/ ./internal/remediation/ ./internal/cli/ -count=1` → ok (5.6 s), ok (1.2 s), ok (3.8 s)
- `go test -fuzz=FuzzScanHCL -fuzztime=30s ./internal/iac/` → 424,664 execs, PASS, and no new testdata/fuzz entries (the one existing entry dates from 18:38)

## Decision (main session) — edge trust vs probe validation naming
`model.Confidence` (verified|inferred|hint) is the only edge-level trust attribute. `Validation` is reserved for probe status per plan v3 G3/AP9: `model.ValidationStatus` (theoretical|validated|refuted|unverifiable) + `ValidationResult{Status, ProbeKind, Evidence, At}`. P1-GRAPH agent asked to apply (remove edge-level `validation` key, add the reserved types). P1-ISSUES must reuse these types.
Noise estate (item 4) delivered: 300 resources, 18 decoy classes, 3 labelled TPs; independence log clean (read only format/schema files + main estate.yaml). Item 5 wiring launched.

- Method: read-only on the repo, no git. ### New defects (severity, location, repro under scratchpad/verify-3/)
1. HIGH — Stack-overflow bypass of the depth limit, an uncatchable crash on 600–800 KB files: full splat chains `a[*][*]…`, and unary runs broken by newlines or comments. `internal/iac/limits.go:129,135-140`. Repro `iac/cliscan/`, `iac/cliscan2/`, `iac/patho_min/`.
2. HIGH — Fail-open CI gate and lifecycle resolve on unparseable files. After a syntax error, a file over 2 MiB, or depth > 256, that file's findings are resolved (when the root has other findings), and `scan --iac --fail-on high` exits 0 with "0 open findings". The IAC_PARSE_LIMIT ERROR is not in the SARIF and does not count for `--fail-on`. Before the fix, the deep file crashed with exit 2, which failed closed. A PR can hide a public bucket by appending 300 nested `[` or 2 MiB of whitespace. `internal/iac/scan.go:533`, `internal/cli/lifecycle.go:38`, `internal/cli/scan.go ~223`. Repro `evade/{ok,bad,size}`, `me/r`.
3. HIGH — IaC finding ids collide across roots (root-relative File): deleting stg/a.tf resolves prod's finding. `internal/iac/scan.go:597-603`. Repro `cli/life/c`.
4. HIGH — `fix --patch` reports EC2_SG_OPEN_RDP/ALL fixed while port 3389 stays world-open (`cli/fix.go:335-353`, `iac/patch.go:638`). Repro `me/fixv`, `cli/fixv`.
5. HIGH — M365 legacy-auth block policy scoped by location, platform or risk, or with app/group exclusions, counts as tenant-wide (`internal/collector/saas/m365.go:225`).
6. MEDIUM — Template `# CLI:` lines allow shell and option injection for aws, az, gcloud and kubectl (`internal/remediation/templates/*.tf|yaml`, e.g. gcp_bucket_public.tf:15, rds_public.tf:18). Repro `iac/cmdinj`.
7. MEDIUM — The PatchTerraform module guard fails open on unresolvable module addresses (`internal/iac/patch.go:99`). Repro `iac/mod3` find_failopen.json.
8. MEDIUM — yor_trace from locals, var, module or built values: the wrong block is patched (`internal/iac/patch.go:364,454`). Repro `iac/trace_local`.
9. MEDIUM — A shared module source is patched for every caller with no warning, and the patch address drops the module prefix (author-flagged, confirmed). Repro `iac/mod3` b0.db.
10. MEDIUM — Redirects leak Authorization and configured headers (`internal/collector/core/rest.go:119`, no CheckRedirect).
11. MEDIUM — A StackSet-only offboard manifest deletes the current org's `cloud-posture` StackSet with no account check (`internal/cli/offboard.go ~351-366`; author-flagged, confirmed).
12. MEDIUM — AdminLevel misses `iam:Put*` and `iam:*` on `arn:aws:iam::*:*` (`internal/collector/aws/policy.go:157,172,200`). GCP: a v4 /0 deny shadows a `::/0` allow (`gcp.go:685`). A same-account second config label is resolved by another label's clean run (store ScopesOf).
13. LOW-MED — Attachment-only offboard manifest (offboard.go ~509). Trivy versioned jar paths and shared k8s images (trivy.go:151).
14. LOW — Symlinked DB risk.json (risk.py:161). Azure protocol-blind specEffective and port overstatement. M365 excludeGuestsOrExternalUsers. Scheme-less webhook URLs (validate.go:363) and the yaml type-error 7-character echo. `/api/findings` invalid min_severity (server.go:435). Migration 5 drops resolved Issue rows. Bad `CLOUD_POSTURE_IAC_MAX_FILE_BYTES` silently defaults (limits.go:40). Symlinked module source (iac.go moduleIndex.resolve). LocateStrict log wording.
15. Still open from verify-2 and not in scope: D6 dynamic blocks; collector ctx-honouring partial results and parent cancel report `ok` (collector.go:191-213); an Azure NSG that was not collected is treated as deny; the empty `[]` ingest error.

### Bottom line
Gates are green. V2-cli closed the small CLI/API defects (doctor, /api/issues, alert, token, choke, dead-end SG, views, Okta), and V2-iac closed D5 and the original D1/D3 repros. But D3 (depth bypass), lifecycle (parse-failure resolve and cross-root ids) and verifyPatch are FAILED, and the three new HIGHs on the IaC CI path (1, 2, 4) mean Phase 0 should not be called done.
- Checker refinements, each red first: in-cell table-row markers (`test_row_marker_inside_last_cell` red, then green); a marker quoted in inline code (`test_marker_text_inside_inline_code_is_not_a_marker` red: the old README text produced about 30 bogus refs, then green); `read-only` treated as a term rather than a superlative (`test_read_only_is_a_term_not_a_superlative` red, then green). The marker body regex never crosses `-->`.
- README rewritten. The first draft failed `make readme-check` with 6 unlinked claims (status box "every", "best-in-class", "tag only", "steps 1 and 2", "2026 trivy-action", "read-only"). Each one was given a marker or reworded. Real lab output for the demo came from a scratchpad build (`go build ./cmd/cloud-posture`, `CLOUD_POSTURE_NO_NETWORK=1`, ingest/paths/plan on `lab/local/out/estate.findings.json` with the lab config) on 2026-10-09.
- Removed from the README as unprovable: "Teams run 45 security tools"; the ✅ comparison table against Prowler/Wiz/Orca/Prisma, including "Aggregates every open-source scanner", "Price $100k+/yr" and "6 hops"; the invented demo output (847 findings, 3 AWS accounts / 12 regions, Azure/GCP/k8s connected); "every feature above is implemented and covered by unit tests" and "Python: 157 tests" (stale); "58 Prowler-style boto3 CIS checks" and "75-check catalog" (no test asserts the counts); "Real-time change detection" (it is polling, now marked experimental); "single pane of glass"; Homebrew, install.sh and docker presented as available today (now "after v0.2.0"); signing and immutability as present fact (now "pipeline configured, no release yet"); "compliance packs" (now "mappings, not certifications; CIS licence pending"). Kept but labelled experimental or maintenance-only: alerts, monitor, Python copilot, cost/ROI, dashboard, Neo4j.
- Also edited: SECURITY.md (new "Correcting a wrong result": rule, fix, affected ids, release; plan v3 §5 "also adopted") and CONTRIBUTING.md (DCO section, readme-check in the gate, governance links).
- docs/metrics.md: north star per plan v3 §8. Query: `"nimbus-closes-paths" in:body is:pr is:merged merged:<Mon>..<Sun> -repo:DreadpiratePickles/nimbus`. The jq sum was checked offline on a sample response (4+2 → 6; null body and a non-integer footer → ignored); the release-download jq was checked on a sample too (archives 5+2 → 7, checksums excluded). Neither the search query nor the other public API calls have run against GitHub (network off): the page says so.
- Gates (P0-GOV scope): `make readme-check` → unittest 28 OK, then "readme-claims: 18 linked claim(s), 0 unlinked, 0 unresolved reference(s): PASS", exit 0. `python/.venv/bin/python -c 'import yaml…'` → "parsed: dco | on: ['pull_request'] | permissions: {'contents': 'read'} | steps: 2". `grep uses:` dco.yml → a single line, `actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1`; unpinned uses lines: 0. DCO harness 7/7 PASS. Relative links and anchors in all 11 edited or new Markdown files resolve (scratchpad `linkcheck.py`: OK). Not run: `make verify` (Go/Python suites untouched by this track; other tracks are editing internal/graph, internal/model and internal/iam concurrently).
- Not done / for others: the DCO GitHub app and branch protection (O4); `tools/northstar/` (P1-PLANDIFF); the post-O3 rename grep (after O3); confirmation of the KEV CC0 and EPSS attribution terms (network off: hedged in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES); the CIS title licence (O6). ## Noise estate measured (item 5) — the honest number
Independent noise estate: 2/3 TPs found, 23/26 reported paths are false positives (precision 11.5%), 34 CRITICAL/HIGH Issues of which 28 on benign resources. Labelled lab unchanged (95.2%). Confirms the red-team member's "self-referential benchmark" warning.
Planning decisions for the engine (binding, in the P1-ENGINE noise-round brief): (1) a path must reach an impact target — exposure-only chains become `Analysis.Exposures`, not paths; (2) exposure requires anonymous reachability (IAM-auth URLs, org-conditioned `Principal *` are private); (3) authenticated LB in front ×0.3, no top priority for non-jewel targets; (4) unusable identities are not paths; (5) catalog alias bug (Azure TLS check → PUBLIC_BLOB) fixed; (6) TP3 miss (DynamoDB sensitive target) fixed. Targets: noise FP ≤ 3, TP1/TP3 ≥ 7, benign CRITICAL/HIGH ≤ 5.
P0-GOV done (governance set, README claim checker, docs/metrics.md). Running: P1-IAM, P1-GRAPH (naming), V3-iac, V3-cli, P1-ENGINE noise round.

- Implemented:
  - `policy.go`: `ParsePolicy`, `Policy`/`Statement`/`PrincipalSet`, and `Ref`/`Layer`.
  - `match.go`: Kleene three-valued logic, glob matching, ARN matching one segment at a time, and principal classification, including `NotPrincipal` identities.
  - `condition.go`: all the String/Numeric/Date/Bool/Binary/IP/Arn/Null operators, `IfExists`, `ForAnyValue`/`ForAllValues`, policy variables, and the catalog of known keys.
  - `evaluate.go`: `Evaluate`/`EvaluateSet`, `Request`, `Decision`, `PolicySet`.
  - `helpers.go`: `IsAdmin`, `AdminLevel`, `AllowedActions`, `ActionCatalog`.
- Green run: `go vet ./internal/iam/` is clean. `go test ./internal/iam/ -count=1 -cover` gives `ok … coverage: 95.4% of statements`.
- Corpus: 147 cases, all passing. By category:

  | category | cases |
  |---|---|
  | deny_precedence | 13 |
  | notaction_notresource | 16 |
  | wildcards | 10 |
  | policy_variables | 11 |
  | conditions | 29 |
  | unknown_keys | 10 |
  | scp_rcp | 12 |
  | boundary | 8 |
  | resource_policy_cross_account | 14 |
  | trust_policy | 10 |
  | kms | 8 |
  | session_policy | 6 |

  Every case has a `source`: an AWS docs URL, or `lab/local/estate.yaml` for the S01 `governing_scps` cases. The corpus contains no PMapper content.
- Required shapes covered:
  - F02: `Allow s3:*` plus `Deny s3:GetObject` gives explicit_deny.
  - S01: the quarantine SCP gives explicit_deny for the batch role and allow for a sibling role.
  - MFA: `Bool` with the key absent gives unknown; `BoolIfExists` gives allow.
  - `aws:PrincipalOrgID`, `aws:SourceVpc`/`SourceVpce`, and IP conditions.
  - KMS without a key policy gives implicit_deny.
  - Cross-account access with and without a resource policy.
- Mutation check: each of these deliberate breakages of `evaluate.go` was caught by failing tests.

  | mutation | failing tests |
  |---|---|
  | unknown treated as allow | 19 |
  | KMS/trust requirement dropped | 4 |
  | SCPs ignored | 6 |
  | cross-account treated as same account | 7 |
  | role-ARN grant not bounded by boundary/session | 3 |

  The file was restored afterwards and is green again.
- Known gaps: Phase-1 category minimums are not met yet (200 cases in total). v1 doesn't model service quirks, VPC endpoint policies, RCP allows, or parity with Simulate/Access Analyzer. The full list is in `docs/iam/evaluator.md`.
- `IsAdmin` versus the collector: 17 of the collector's 18 cases agree. The two deliberate differences are documented.

Fix: limits.go `measureNesting` (per-bracket levels; unary/splat frames held per term across newlines/comments and nested brackets; `for` braces newline-insensitive; matching-pair pops only; separate chain measure for binary ops and index/attribute steps). Green: 35/35 shapes rejected, 22 benign shapes accepted. FuzzScanHCL now asserts tree depth ≤ 4·(depth+chain)+8 on everything the guard accepts, seeded with both full-size repros and 24 small shapes.

## P1-ISSUES item 10 (Opus agent)

Scope: path-level Issues with stable ids (plan v3 §9 item 10, AP3/RP5/RP7). Files touched only in
`internal/store/{migrate.go,issues.go,store.go (issuesSummarySQL const + its EnsureViews entry)}`, new
`internal/store/{path_issues.go,path_issues_test.go,migrate6_test.go,testdata/schema_v5.sql}`, `internal/model/issue.go`,
new `internal/model/issue_path_test.go`, `internal/store/viewsql_test.go`, `python/nimbus/store.py` (views only),
`python/tests/test_views_lifecycle.py`. Did not touch lifecycle.go, internal/cli, attackpath, risk, catalog, iac.

Red first: model/store tests failed to compile (undefined PathIssueID / model.IssuePath / RebuildPathIssues); pytest
`test_view_sql_matches_go` + `test_python_rebuilds_issues_summary_on_go_db` failed; after the first implementation
`TestSuppressedRequiresReasonAndExpiry` and `TestSuppressedSingleIssueSurvivesRebuildAndExpires` failed on the suppression
CHECK (SQLite checks an UPSERT's would-be-inserted row before resolving the conflict) — fixed by insert/update split
(path) and carrying reason/expires_at into the insert row (RebuildIssues).

- Migration 6 `path_issues` (`migrate.go` `issuesV6Schema`, `migratePathIssues`): rebuilds `issues` (CHECK cannot be
  altered) with `kind IN ('single','combination','path','chokepoint','correlated')`, adds `entry_id NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`,
  `anchor_id, rule_id, secondary_key, secondary_keys, path_ids, score, band, confidence_label (verified|inferred|hint),
  validation_json DEFAULT '{"status":"theoretical"}', weak_link, toxic_combination, reason, expires_at, approved_by,
  suppressed_at, verified_at, verified_by`; table CHECK `state <> 'suppressed' OR (reason AND expires_at)`;
  UNIQUE widened to `(kind, canonical_id, resource_id, entry_id)`; new `path_issue_history(issue_id, state, at, scan_id,
  score, secondary_key)`. Idempotent (skips rebuild when DDL already admits 'path'); drops `issues_summary` before the
  rename (EnsureViews recreates it). Deviation: the existing `issues.confidence` is REAL (source agreement), so the
  `model.Confidence` label lives in `confidence_label`.
- Ids (`model/issue.go`): `PathIssueID(kind, entry, anchor, rule) = hex(sha256(kind|entry|anchor|rule)[:16])`;
  `PathIssueKey(p)`: entry = identity of a relation-"entry" first step, else `internet`; anchor = last hop resource;
  rule = RuleID, `GRAPH_TRAVERSAL`/"" → `traversal`. `PathSecondaryKey(p)` = hash of entry|rule|full hop list.
  `PathConfidence` = weakest edge `confidence` (unlabelled edge → inferred). `ScoreBand` = risk.BandFor thresholds (pinned).
- `Store.RebuildPathIssues(scanID, *attackpath.Analysis, now) (*PathIssueResult, error)`: `an.Paths` only (Incomplete and
  any Exposures ignored); score = max over the Issue's paths; resolve/reopen preserving first_seen; suppressed stays until
  expiry, then reopens; history on state change or secondary-key change. `SuppressIssue(id, Suppression{Reason, ExpiresAt,
  ApprovedBy}, now)` → `*SuppressionError` / `ErrIssueNotFound`; `PathIssueHistory(id)`. `RebuildIssues` now only reads
  single/combination (it used to resolve every other row) and reopens expired suppressions. `IssueCounts` adds
  `Suppressed` and `Paths{Total,Open,Resolved,Suppressed,BySeverity,ByBand}`; top-level counts stay finding-derived.
- `issues_summary` gains `max_score`; Go `issuesSummarySQL` == Python `ISSUES_SUMMARY_SQL` (drift tests both sides);
  Python recreates it only when the issues table has migration-6 columns.

Evidence: `go vet ./internal/store/ ./internal/model/ && go test ./internal/store/ ./internal/model/ -count=1` ok;
`cd python && .venv/bin/pytest -q` 233 passed (before the docstring tweak; rerun below). `go test ./... -count=1`: 33 ok, 1 FAIL
`internal/remediation` `TestTemplateCLILinesQuoteHostileNames` (V3-iac's package, unrelated to this change). internal/cli,
internal/server, tests/groundtruth green.

Integration pass (internal/cli, not done here): after `attackpath.Traverse` on the full stored graph in scan/ingest, call
`st.RebuildPathIssues(scanID, an, now)` next to `RebuildIssues`; `/api/summary` can expose `IssueCounts().Paths`.
Open: scope-aware resolution (a partial-account scan currently resolves path Issues it did not traverse) — callers must
pass the whole-estate analysis.

Judgment calls / not done:
- Gate severity for scan gaps follows this track's spec (HIGH), so `--fail-on critical` does not trip on a gap. V3-iac's `output.GateBlocks` blocks at every threshold and is not used by the CLI gate. One of the two should be chosen.
- M365: up to 2 individually excluded users are still tenant-wide (Microsoft's emergency-access guidance). The spec said "no exclusions"; groups, roles, guests and apps are refused.
- The Scope label and the root-scoped ids for checkov/trivy change ids/scopes once. Findings stored before this change (no account_label, old checkov ids) are not resolved by absence in the new scopes, and old external-tool rows are left open until resolved by hand or by a re-observation.
- `fix --auto-pr` now drops unverified patches as `--require-verify` does: a PR presents its patches as fixes.
- Not touched: the M365 token POST could follow a 307 with the client secret (it uses its own http.Client, not REST). This is not in scope; noting it.
